# Upstream contributions

Current overview and priorities: [inventory](inventory.md).

These are scoped upstream changes, not extra Lowrail request prices or whole-application speed claims.

**Checked on 2026-10-22:** every merged/open label below was verified with
`gh pr view`, including the PR body and GitHub's merge commit when merged.
Later checks are dated on the individual entries.
Numbers summarize the linked PR's own evidence, not new measurements.
Allocation reductions, instruction counts, fallback diagnostics and helper
microbenchmarks are not interchangeable with request latency or throughput.
Where a PR reports no performance measurement, none is inferred here.

**Rechecked on 2026-11-02:** every PR state below was read again with
`gh pr view`; PRs opened October 23 – November 2 were added from
`gh search prs --author thomasklemm --created 2026-10-23..2026-11-02`.

Coverage: public PRs by `thomasklemm` opened October 19 – November 2 that
relate to Lowrail, plus the older Campfire work below. Roundhouse compiler work
and a review of another author's Mail PR are listed separately. Closed
superseded PRs are omitted except for the notes under [Closed](#closed).

[Counts](#counts-2026-11-27) · [Merged](#merged) · [Closed](#closed) · [Open](#open) · [Campfire maintenance](#campfire-maintenance) ·
[Reviews](#reviews) · [Roundhouse](#roundhouse) ·
[Investigated, not submitted](#investigated-not-submitted)

## Counts (2026-11-27)

Every pull request by `thomasklemm` outside `argos-ri/argos` and his own repositories, read on 2026-11-27 at 19:11 UTC
through GraphQL search (641 PRs, paginated; [Lowrail-era list](data/contributions-2026-11-27.tsv)):

```sh
gh api graphql -f q='author:thomasklemm is:pr -repo:argos-ri/argos -user:thomasklemm' -f query='
  query($q: String!, $c: String) { search(query: $q, type: ISSUE, first: 50, after: $c) {
    pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
    nodes { ... on PullRequest { repository { nameWithOwner } number title state isDraft createdAt mergedAt closedAt } } } }'
```

**What counts** (Thomas, 2026-11-27): every PR created on or after 2026-10-19, Lowrail's first commit, in an
open-source repository is Lowrail work, and every one of them has an entry in this ledger. Left out: `roundhouse-rb/*`
(Roundhouse, its own project: 44 PRs since 2026-10-19, 37 merged, 7 closed; see [Roundhouse](#roundhouse)),
`argos-ri/*` and `thomasklemm/*`. fables-tales/rubyfmt (7), matz/spinel (6) and roundhouse-rb/once-campfire (3) stay
out: all 16 were created between 2026-05-10 and 2026-10-06, before Lowrail, and are Roundhouse and Spinel compiler work
(formatter fixes, Spinel type inference, the Roundhouse preview stack of Campfire). The older Campfire PRs from
2026-10-05 to 10-09 are listed under [Campfire maintenance](#campfire-maintenance) but not counted. PRs created before
2026-10-19 are not in the ledger. Merged means GitHub's `mergedAt`; ankane/blind_index#62 counts as closed although
its change landed as ankane's commit. Draft is an open draft; Open is open and ready.

## Merged

All entries in this section are **merged; rechecked on 2026-11-02**.
The merge links use GitHub's `mergeCommit`, not the contributor's pre-merge commit.

- [rails/rails#59662](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59662) (OP8, [app suites](app-suites.md#openproject)), keep models with mutable defaults off the frozen defaults (unreleased change from #59083). **Ready** (marked 2026-11-10 by `script/pr-queue ready OP8` on Thomas's decision, no comment; 15 of 15 non-draft slots now used), opened 2026-11-05 as a draft. **2026-11-14, still needed after [#59141](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59141)** (tenderlove, merged 2026-11-11), which moved Active Record attributes into Arrays and rewrote the default path OP8 touches. New records now read their defaults through `build_from_initialize` and `LazyIndexedAttributeSet#default_attribute`, which still dups the shared default `Attribute` from `build_default_attributes`; that set is still frozen, and so cast at schema load, only when every attribute is `freezable?`. On plain main `50c54eed06` both bugs reproduce: OP8's two tests fail on SQLite and PostgreSQL, [serialized_jsonb_default_insert.rb](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/serialized_jsonb_default_insert.rb) raises `PG::NotNullViolation`, [op8_probe2.rb](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/op8_probe2.rb) saves the unread default as NULL and shares the nested default, and [op8_probe3.rb](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/op8_probe3.rb) shows nested jsonb, `text[]` and hstore defaults shared, STI subclass included. With OP8 every probe line matches 8-1-stable `71e963ed1b`, and models with only scalar or NULL defaults keep read-only defaults. Rebased with no conflicts and an identical patch (range-diff `=`), force-pushed with a lease on `490fdf855b`: head [`71c77e3a01`](https://github.com/thomasklemm/rails/commit/71c77e3a013680390154ef9f8390c263e9fed126). Ruby 4.0.9, PostgreSQL 17: AR SQLite **10,379 runs** and PostgreSQL **11,302 runs** with no failures except the `secure_password` timing test (green alone) and 4 `sqlite_rake_test` errors from a missing `sqlite3` CLI (green once installed); Active Model **1,269 runs**; RuboCop **3,581 files, no offenses**. Reverting any of the four production lines fails OP8's tests. Callgrind on the new base, median of 3 per tree with identical lockfiles ([matrix5.sh](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/op8-bench/matrix5.sh), [results](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/op8-bench/results-50c54eed06.tsv)): `Model.new` plus first read with scalar defaults **−0.01%**, `'{}'` JSON defaults **+18.94%**, nested JSON default **+22.26%**, A/A 0.00%, so the body's 18–22% still holds. Body unchanged: its `bdb3d96012` links still describe main (the same lines are now `schema_context.rb` L137–L139). Thomas author and committer, no trailers, no comment; new-head CI green (Buildkite 136616, docs-preview, rails-new-docker, labeler), merge state clean ([queue item](../../bench/upstream/pr-queue/items/OP8.md)). **2026-11-18 ([rules review](rules-review-2026-11-18-rails-ruby.md)):** guard and serializer comment kept; body: 'on an earlier revision' cut in the item, not PATCHed. **2026-11-18 (rules review, thermos-sol confirm):** body shortened per Leave out and PATCHed. **2026-11-27, rafaelfranca asked inline** whether to override `freezable?` in the types instead of passing the value: his shape is better now that [#59972](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59972) deep-dups frozen defaults, so only `Type::Serialized` refuses. Built as [`424441d986`](https://github.com/thomasklemm/rails/commit/424441d98683697154e5a3b85de8fc86fefec508) on a new fork branch (PR branch untouched), thermos-sol approves; Callgrind ([results](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/op8-bench/results-9e82904f18.tsv)) keeps json `{}` and NULL json models at main's cost (OP8 +6.82% on json `{}`), and serialized models cost the same as OP8. Reply, title and body drafts in [op8-rafael-reply.md](../../bench/upstream/app_suites/op8-rafael-reply.md), waiting for Thomas. **Merged 2026-11-27 15:27 UTC** by rafaelfranca ([15a91fed06](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/15a91fed06)).
- [rails/rails#59671](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59671) (E2, [ecosystem tests](ecosystem-tests.md)), the schema readers find SQLite tables by any ASCII case of their name. **Ready** (marked by Thomas 2026-11-08 21:11 UTC), stacked on #59668, opened 2026-11-05, head `63d1880397`, Buildkite green. scarroll32 asked on 2026-11-08 20:36 UTC to be pinged on it when it merges, so ransack issue 1799 can re-enable its case-insensitive SQLite table spec. **2026-11-18 ([rules review](rules-review-2026-11-18-rails-ruby.md)):** candidate `-lean` 9bf31a08d7 drops one test comment (SQLite adapter 237 runs, 0 F); not pushed. **2026-11-18 (rules review, thermos-sol approve):** lease-pushed head [`9bf31a08d7`](https://github.com/thomasklemm/rails/commit/9bf31a08d7) (one test comment dropped); body updated. **2026-11-18 15:00 UTC:** Buildkite 136807 red on that head in rubocop (Lint/Syntax in guides/source/wishlists.md) and actioncable 4.0 (four Socket/Stream tests), neither touched by the change; main green since 136818. No rebase pushed. Thomas: leave it as is, no rebase for CI alone. **Merged 2026-11-27 15:57 UTC** by rafaelfranca ([b79cd26169](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/b79cd26169)). **Follow-up 2026-11-27 17:02 UTC:** after both thermos-sol and Astra approved the text in round 2, posted a short note on the closed tracking issue [activerecord-hackery/ransack#1799](https://github.com/activerecord-hackery/ransack/issues/1799#issuecomment-7223967811): the fix is on Rails `main`; the remaining checklist steps are to check `rails-main`, uncomment the spec and confirm the job stays green.
- [rails/rails#60006](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/60006) (ATF1), `ActionText::Attachment.from_node` again receives `nil` for attachables the bulk lookup didn't locate, so an app's override of it applies again (unreleased regression from #58173; Campfire's `ContentFiltersTest` fails 6 of 19 tests on main). - [rails/rails#60007](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/60007) (SP1), upgrade-guide note that `default_url_options` is a frozen `{}` unless configured (#58483), which breaks code setting a host in place, as Spree's mailers do. - [rails/rails#59617](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59617) (TC-1 from the [census backlog](census-backlog.md#tc-1-keep-the-utc-offset-in-timechange-for-times-with-a-zone-object)), `Time#change` on a `Time` with a zone object keeps the original UTC offset when the zone gives the new local time that offset. Correctness fix: on 8.1 and main, TZInfo zones raised `TZInfo::AmbiguousTime` in the hour repeated when clocks are set back (also in Active Model's datetime cast when precision truncation is needed), and `ActiveSupport::TimeZone` picked the other occurrence in Dublin; the differential over every tzdata transition up to 2037 differs only there. Cost on zone-object Times: +6–12% instructions interpreted, +16–22% YJIT, about 2× when the offset changes. Reviews: subagent primary and adversarial approve, outsider body rewrite, Sol "open after fixes" (applied). L1. - [ruby/date#199](https://github.com/ruby/date/pull/199) (Ruby 3 from the [Rails 8.2 differential](rails-82-differential.md#changed-and-nan-a-rubydate-bug)), `Date#<=>` returns `nil` for a NaN (`Float::NAN`, `BigDecimal("NaN")`) instead of raising, so `Date == NaN` is `false`. Correctness fix, no measurement. Opened 2026-11-04, ready for review, head `f1d893f834`; CI awaits approval, no reply yet. **Comment 2026-11-14 02:18 UTC** by jeremyevans: the description's claim that `Date < NaN` raises `ArgumentError` "as for Integer" is wrong, since `1 < Float::NAN` and `1 < BigDecimal("NaN")` both return false ([comment](https://github.com/ruby/date/pull/199#issuecomment-6870069360)). He is right: Integer and Float define their own `<`, while Date, like Time and Rational, gets `<` from Comparable, which raises when `<=>` returns `nil` (on Ruby 4.0.7, `Time.now < Float::NAN` and `Rational(1) < Float::NAN` both raise). **Corrected 2026-11-14**: the description now says `Date < NaN` still raises `ArgumentError` "as `Comparable` does for `Time` and `Rational`", with the rest of the body unchanged; code, tests and head untouched. [Reply](https://github.com/ruby/date/pull/199#issuecomment-6874184589) agrees and says the description is fixed. **Reworked 2026-11-14**, head `8a34d5a595` (two commits on master `2c48b11dde`), force-pushed with a lease on `f1d893f834`; no comment posted. The old head returned `ajd <=> other` unchanged, which broke the documented -1/0/1/nil contract: with a Numeric subclass whose `coerce` returns `[N.new(r), self]` and whose `<=>` returns `r`, `Date <=> N.new(5)` was 1 on master and 5 on the PR (-3 and 0.5 likewise). Commit 1 (`c125a1d54d`) returns `nil` when `ajd <=> other` does and passes everything else through `rb_cmpint`, as before; its tests check `<=>` (nil) and `==` (false) against `Float::NAN` for Date and a noon-UTC DateTime (Integer ajd) and add one normalization assertion (custom `<=>` of 5 gives 1). It drops the questioned `assert_raise(ArgumentError) { d < nan }` (Comparable, not this change), the `BigDecimal("NaN")` case (same `nil` path) and `gem "bigdecimal"` from the Gemfile. Commit 2 (`8a34d5a595`) came from the skeptic pass: `step`, `upto` and `downto` passed `Date#<=>` to `FIX2INT`, so a NaN limit now gave `TypeError` on 64-bit Linux instead of master's `ArgumentError`, and by the Ruby 4.0 header (`RB_FIX2INT` is an unchecked shift where `int` and `long` have the same size) an endless `downto` on Windows; master already had this for non-Numeric limits such as `Object.new`. It uses `rb_cmpint`, and its test checks `upto`, `downto`, `step(limit, 1)` and `step(limit, -1)` raise `ArgumentError` for `Float::NAN` and `Object.new` with blocks that `flunk` if they yield; mutants (positive-step branch back on `FIX2INT`, `downto` or `upto` reading nil as positive via `FIX2LONG`) each fail fast. **Probe** (Ruby 4.0.7, bigdecimal 4.0.1 outside the bundle; `<=>`, `==`, `<`, `>`, `>=`, `eql?`, `===` for Date, noon-UTC DateTime and +02:00 DateTime against Integer, Bignum, Rational, Float incl. ±0.0, ±Infinity and NaN, BigDecimal incl. ±Infinity and NaN, the custom Numeric with 5, -3, 0.5, nil and 0, Time, nil, a String, Date and DateTime): against master only `Float::NAN`, `BigDecimal("NaN")` and the custom nil change (`<=>` nil, `==` false, `<`/`>`/`>=` still `ArgumentError`, now "comparison of Date with …"); 5, -3, 0.5 and 0 give 1, -1, 1 and 0 as on master, where the old head gave 5, -3 and 0.5; everything else identical. **Tests**: `bundle exec rake compile test` passes on 4.0.7, 4.0.9, ruby master (4.1.0dev `b7e8fad022`) and 2.6.10, the gemspec minimum (151 tests; one Ractor omission on 2.6); the NaN test errors on master and the normalization assertion fails on the old head (`<1> expected but was <5>`). Fork CI on the head passed every job, Windows (mingw, mswin) and TruffleRuby included; upstream CI awaits approval. **Passes**: skeptic subagent on the one-commit rework: changes (the loops above; nits applied: no `ajd` local, DateTime at noon UTC). Sol on the two-commit head before last: changes, applied (`step(limit, 1)`, flunking blocks, commit 2 message states only the verified `TypeError`, body says "the comparisons in"); asserting `d < Float::NAN` skipped by Thomas as Comparable behaviour. No pass on `8a34d5a595` yet. **Body**: paragraph 1 no longer quotes the error message (for `BigDecimal("NaN")` master raises "comparison of Rational with BigDecimal failed", not "… with NaN …"); paragraph 2 says it returns `nil` when `ajd <=> other` does and still normalizes other values to -1, 0 or 1, drops the bigdecimal sentence and adds that the second commit makes the comparisons in `step`, `upto` and `downto` raise `ArgumentError` for a limit `Date#<=>` can't compare, instead of passing `nil` to `FIX2INT`. **2026-11-15 (red-PR sweep):** conflicted with master `6f788dd` (wtn's new tests, two DateTime offset asserts at the end of `test__compare`). Rebased keeping both sides' added lines unchanged; our +/- lines and both messages unchanged. Head [`1c273d40cc`](https://github.com/thomasklemm/date/commit/1c273d40ccfea71f8a9db0fc3fe9b4111cd8fb73), force-pushed with a lease on `8a34d5a595`; `rake compile test` on 4.0.7: 156 tests, 0 failures. Mergeable; upstream CI still `action_required` (a maintainer must approve the workflow run). **Merged 2026-11-17 02:16 UTC** by jeremyevans ([e743163baa](https://github.com/ruby/date/commit/e743163baa)).

- [#59533](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59533), build `WhereClause#ast` predicates in one pass — one Array fewer per compiled where clause, interpreter and YJIT alike. Allocation-only. **Merged 2026-11-02.** [Merge b5be036](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/b5be03636e0ac5c3d763411b8be9300e589a0953).

- [#59316](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59316), URL generation skips temporary Strings and Regexp captures for plain hosts and the common UTF-8 `http`/`https` protocols, and allocates a host buffer only when the host is rewritten; parser fallbacks stay for embedded schemes or ports, String subclasses and proxies. Resource URL helper **16 → 9** objects per call in all three modes; helper microbenchmark IPS 1.22× / 1.36× / 1.23× (interpreter / YJIT / ZJIT, Ruby 4.1.0dev), not request throughput. +95/−13. Opened 2026-10-23. **Merged** by amatsuda on 2026-10-24 02:34 UTC (merge commit `10eee71222`). Not listed here before 2026-11-04.
- [#59329](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59329), general route generation finds the retained route-part prefix with `rindex` and `take` instead of a `reverse_each.drop_while` chain — `issue_path("acme", "launch", "417", page: 3)` **35 → 32** objects; optimized helpers unchanged. +8/−5. Opened 2026-10-23. **Merged** by amatsuda on 2026-10-24 02:42 UTC (merge commit `843fa132cb`). Not listed here before 2026-11-04.
- [#59584](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59584), before Ruby 4.1 `JSONGemCoderEncoder` escapes by searching for each of the five characters with `include?` instead of one `gsub!` over a Regexp union — `posts.to_json` −4.5% / −6.1% instructions (interp / YJIT), jbuilder −2.9% / −6.1%, `render json:` before 8.1 −6.0% / −7.0%, text with `<b>`/`&` −14% / −15%; ruby-bench railsbench YJIT −0.28% and −0.76% in two runs (A/A −0.08% / +0.04%), the rest within noise; Ruby ≤ 4.0 only (4.1 takes `tr!`), so no ZJIT number (bet S1, [record](serialization-cpu.md)). History: the full variant (`e13f3f6`, both encoders, bound-method guards) went through six Sol + adversarial Astra rounds (subclasses, SafeBuffer, singleton `include?`/`equal?`/`force_encoding`, proxies, pre-4.0 Ractor IsolationError) and was held after fatkodima's objection on #59577; replaced by the coder-only variant, which no custom `stringify` can reach. Sol approve on both rubrics; Astra: settings read before `force_encoding` changed error precedence for a frozen String, fixed (main's order); accepted: a prepended `String#gsub!` sees two String-pattern calls instead of one Regexp call (same bytes). Lib +9/−8, L1. Head `8f77e33`, `fa18f3fbf9` on 2026-11-04 (change for amatsuda's request, in flight). Opened 2026-11-03. amatsuda (2026-11-04 00:55 UTC) measured `escape!` slower on small documents with five `include?` scans; our reply (06:11 UTC) kept the single regexp `gsub!` when JS separators are escaped too and his three direct checks only when they are off (the `load_defaults 8.1` default), head `fa18f3fbf9`, title now "Escape HTML entities from JSON::Coder with direct checks". amatsuda (07:23 UTC) suggested applying the same pattern to `JS_SEPARATORS` (two `include?`-guarded `gsub!` for U+2028/U+2029) — measured: separate `gsub!` calls get slower once the document has characters to escape (his version 5,169 vs 2,999 ns on main with YJIT on a short document with `&`, `<b>`, U+2028 and U+2029). Head `fc24e18ab8` (2026-11-04) keeps main's single `gsub!` and skips it only when `include?` finds none of `<`, `>`, `&`, with JS separators off: `{"id":1,"name":"x"}` 354 → 278 ns, 11-column record 759 → 328 ns (YJIT); a short document with characters to escape is the same in the interpreter and 10% slower with YJIT (2,316 → 2,550 ns; Callgrind +2.7% / +2.3%); JS separators on unchanged. CI green; [reply](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59584#issuecomment-6627536108) posted 09:34 UTC. 2026-11-05: amatsuda asked for single-character `&`/`<`/`>` test cases (each `include?` check was untested); added, amended to head `256dd9ee83`, replied. 2026-11-05 00:31: amatsuda asked for single-character `&`/`<`/`>` test cases; added (`256dd9ee83`). Approved ("LGTM") and **merged 2026-11-05 01:41 UTC** by amatsuda ([3b2702a](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/3b2702adea5bdd2b537e9fa64267b3db6f13689f)). **Reverted 2026-11-09 09:46 UTC** by byroot in [#59713](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59713): the benchmark covered only small documents; on large strings the three `include?` scans are worse than one regexp match once the string no longer fits in CPU caches, and the path only matters before Ruby 4.1, which uses `String#tr`.
- [#59634](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59634) (AR-4 from the [API regression audit](api-regressions.md)), `where!` and `having!` accept bind values again. Opened by the queue 2026-11-05 01:53 UTC. kamipo pushed `c8490688d7` (02:58: `where!`/`having!` keep their splat signatures as wrappers around new `_where!`/`_having!`, which take an array; internal callers use the underscore versions) and commented: "direct calls to `where!` are supported again, but overriding private APIs is at the gem's own risk." Approved and **merged 2026-11-05 03:25 UTC** by kamipo ([06eae38](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/06eae38e6e9987f4379e9aaf3e79e925d9e1cb39)).
- [#59640](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59640) (SU1), allow anonymous subclasses of cache stores with LocalCache. Opened by the queue 2026-11-05 06:53 UTC. Approved ("Looks good.") and **merged 2026-11-05 08:20 UTC** by amatsuda ([f9c9402](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/f9c94027214d14d9076e6bfed5ba3ac956486cd7)). Follow-up 2026-11-09: byroot asked why the class name is in the key at all when `object_id` is unique, and merged [#59714](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59714) ("Further simplify LocalCache#local_cache_key") at 09:59 UTC.
- [#59570](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/59570), `OutputBuffer#<<` runs the escape steps inline instead of `ERB::Util.unwrapped_html_escape` — per append 141 → 110 ns / 68 → 52 ns; erubi-rails −5.8% / −5–6% instructions. Boundary, left to the reviewer: modules prepended to `ERB::Util`'s singleton class no longer see template appends (Astra showed this also holds for prepends after load, so the description says so). 2026-11-05 09:23: byroot noted the second `to_s` was redundant; dropped (`043143393b`). **Merged 2026-11-05 09:34 UTC** by byroot ([bdb3d96](https://github.com/rails/rails/commit/bdb3d960121e7eb73a61c04099d1eef3c821e397)).

**Combined (2026-11-03):** all eleven merged #59533–#59544 together, against the same Rails main with them
reverted, remove 1.66% / 1.64% of allocations (interpreter / YJIT) over 206 workloads in sixteen apps; wall time on
railsbench and lobsters is a tie within A/A ([combined effect](combined-effect.md)).

**Combined, round 3 (2026-11-17):** 56 of the 61 merged PRs reverted together on main `4500a94c34` remove 5.48% / 5.38%
of census allocations (interpreter / YJIT, 104 workloads in eleven apps) on Ruby 4.0.9 and 5.86% / 5.79% (interpreter /
ZJIT) on Ruby master ([round 3](combined-effect.md#round-3-every-merged-pr-on-rails-main-2026-11-17)).

### Ruby — ruby/ruby

- [ruby/ruby#19680](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19680), ZJIT caches `iseq_may_write_block_code()`; fixes Shopify/ruby#1064. k0kubun asked for a cache that is dropped after each compile and for evidence in the tools' own format. Reworked that way, it saves about 1.2% of compile instructions on railsbench, lobsters and shipit (Callgrind, A/A within 0.1%), and a 2,000-call method compiles in 3.68 G instead of 7.15 G instructions, but `run_benchmarks.rb` shows no difference beyond A/A. Closed with the raw tables ([closing comment](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19680#issuecomment-6624629962)). The railsbench GC effect found while measuring it is recorded as lead G in the [lead queue](zjit-iseq-leads.md#queue-which-leads-are-free-2026-11-03). **Reopened 2026-11-17 22:27 UTC** by tekknolagi; new head `3e63efefe6` (cache used only when the ISEQ already has a payload), our comment 2026-11-18 00:43 UTC. k0kubun asked 2026-11-18 01:08 UTC whether `IseqPayload` grows and suggested the bit-field idea from #19962. **2026-11-18 ([rules review](rules-review-2026-11-18-rails-ruby.md)):** item sections tidied; live body unchanged; waiting on k0kubun after today's size reply. **Merged again 2026-11-19 18:00 UTC** by k0kubun at `3e63efefe6`, after his approval: "Alright, that settles it, for now at least. Thank you."
**Merged; checked on 2026-11-02.** JIT correctness and diagnostics fixes; none claims a speedup.

- [#19485](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19485), stop ZJIT send counters accumulating across specialization retries — repro counters **[18, 18, 9] → [9, 9, 9]**; railsbench's first two counters **749,107 → 250,038**, control unchanged. Counter-correctness fix; three stats-off chunky-png comparisons ran 2.3–5.7% longer with identical HIR, cause unexplained, **no performance claim**. **Merged 2026-10-29.** [Merge 8258233](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/8258233785195eb92e05e7bd964d0b00d94ed824).
- [#19538](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19538), exit with the original send's stack on ZJIT stack overflow — master crashed or returned wrong arguments for default-filled/reordered keywords, rest and `&nil` sends at all 12 tested VM stack sizes; the PR passes all of them. Correctness fix, normal path unchanged. **Merged 2026-10-29.** [Merge 461085c](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/461085c2c7c23a0c3393223d603d4254f30f6f7c).
- [#19554](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19554), YJIT falls back for explicit keywords in IFUNC block calls — `enum_for` with `yield [], key: 1` returned `[[[], 1]]` instead of the interpreter's `[[[], {key: 1}]]`. Correctness fix. **Merged 2026-10-29.** [Merge 54d0988](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/54d098816777fd29e716746c2adcb3bbe4f88710).
- [#19586](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19586), ZJIT guards the lookup CME when resolving aliases — re-aliasing a module method could keep calling the old target; regressions for sends and `super` fail without the fix. **Merged 2026-10-29.** [Merge 1654a6a](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/1654a6a0fd71ef962635d1322a37cc50f908d91a).
- [#19619](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19619), backport of #19554 to `ruby_4_0`, requested in review. **Merged 2026-10-30.** [Merge 9bebcb4](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/9bebcb455f0f9ddbd4af1d3e5dcf2a96b508a455).
- [#19484](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19484), allow Proc arguments for explicit block parameters in ZJIT — **27 final send fallbacks/request removed in Fizzy, none in the blog**; earlier Arel-style forwarding micro ZJIT **−61.41% [−63.78, −59.53]**, interpreter **+1.75% [+0.03, +3.67]**, YJIT unresolved. Nine unrelated yield fallbacks remain; no request-throughput claim. **Merged 2026-11-03** by XrXr ([13c3f56](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/13c3f56bb85f7e3f7e07b945c829117b48ee43f5)).
- [#19660](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19660), `Hash.new` redefinition and prepend are no longer ignored: #19537 registered `BOP_NEW` on Hash's singleton class, which `vm_redefinition_check_flag()` didn't map, so `opt_new` kept its Hash fast path after `Hash.new` was redefined. Correctness fix with a regression test; Codex Thermos (GPT-6 Astra) approve on both rubrics. Opened 2026-11-03. **Merged 2026-11-03** by byroot ([a8b095a](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/a8b095a07e55adf429839d4642027e832e30d3a7)); follow-ups #19661 and #19663 are under [Open](#open).
- [#19699](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19699), test-only fix for our flaky macOS test `test_send_proc_block_arg_to_block_param_callee` (from merged #19484): insta's first snapshot runs `cargo metadata`, and the child's SIGCHLD leaves a trap interrupt that makes the inlined second run side-exit. Both programs now run before the first snapshot. A/B on `macos-26`, 8 shards: test alone 8/1200 → 0/1200 failures, in the full suite 5/32 → 0/32. 2026-11-04: tekknolagi asked to bucket both runs into one concatenated snapshot; updated to one commit `a6189df0cb`, approved by tekknolagi and XrXr. **Merged 2026-11-05 00:07 UTC** by XrXr ([a47dd64](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/a47dd64f766726f492ac242959f139721b9f3ab9)).
- [#19678](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19678), ZJIT interpolates Fixnums and Modules without sending `to_s` (`rb_fix_to_s` without a frame, `rb_mod_name`), guarded like a cfunc call; fixes Shopify/ruby#1091. ZJIT per iteration: `to_s` sends at interpolation 4,270 → 0 lobsters, 3,795 → 0 shipit; objects beyond the interpreter 1,576 → 931 / 3,835 → 1,361. Allocation counts, no time claim. tekknolagi's four review comments addressed in `3656855d29`; head `b6ed2f484e`, `4944f1a29e` on 2026-11-04 (refactor push for k0kubun's 02:51 UTC review: one if-else chain, each branch with its own lookup, guards and codegen; our 06:13 UTC reply offers a helper for the repeated setup). No answer by 09:00 UTC; CI green apart from the slow Haiku job. Opened 2026-11-03. Approved and **merged 2026-11-05 01:31 UTC** by k0kubun ([8161a1f](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/8161a1f6c2ddaf9a9c1a280dbd64aa6b7ad698c7)).
- [#19682](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19682), ZJIT records in the `invokesuper` profile whether the frame had a block, so `super` from a method that got a block no longer side-exits on every call; for Shopify/ruby#1100. Side exits per iteration 2,052 / 341 / 57 → 0 (railsbench / lobsters / shipit); repro 1,090 → 942 Ir per call; ruby-bench Ir within A/A. Opened 2026-11-03. On 2026-11-04, after k0kubun's two review points, it skips the frame's block when the call site passes its own (`super(&b)`, `super { }`) and keeps the flag in a `bool` on `ProfileEntry` instead of a `HashSet`: `zjit_alloc_bytes` railsbench 7,456,668 → 7,328,524 (base 7,357,592), lobsters 20,111,412 → 19,855,120 (base 19,866,920); an extra slot in the operand profile measured 7,354,212 / 19,904,604 and needs a special case in HIR. Head `0442275697` (one commit); replies posted on both review comments. k0kubun approved at 02:37 UTC ("I'd like to land a fix for the permanent exit"); CI green on `0442275697`; no answer to the 06:20 UTC replies by 09:00 UTC. Rethought on 2026-11-04 ([zjit-super-block.md](zjit-super-block.md)): keep it as it is; two measured limits (sites that only sometimes get a block, ISEQs with no version left) and the larger `super` gaps are recorded there as leads. Approved and **merged 2026-11-05 01:51 UTC** by k0kubun ([8b9dc08](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/8b9dc082e7c28bc464aa3f61b88585751accee76)); his review note accepts the padding bool for now but calls it temporary until profiles get a more compact representation.
- [#19620](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19620), `[3.4]` backport of merged #19554 to `ruby_3_4` (YJIT falls back for explicit keywords in IFUNC block calls), requested in review. Merged by nagachika on 2026-11-06 13:16 UTC as `9350473ec8`.
- [#19648](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19648), YJIT checks a singleton class's attached-object identity in C (`rb_yjit_attached_object_is()`), so compiling the guard can't raise `Ractor::IsolationError`; reworked to XrXr's request (#19740 fixes the Ractor test failure). Head `a9b63e5264`. XrXr approved 2026-11-05 23:27 UTC ("ty"). **Merged 2026-11-06 00:24 UTC** by XrXr ([f0de939f61](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/f0de939f61)).
- [#19909](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19909) (ZNE1, [record](zjit-not-equal.md)), ZJIT specializes a polymorphic `opt_neq` per profiled receiver class by sharing the other `opt_*` sends' arm in the HIR builder; answers skipkayhil's close of rails/rails#59811 ("Let's fix this in the JITs?"). `neq_nil_symbol` 4.58× (4.4× to 5.1× in three rounds), polymorphic sends per iteration lobsters 10,816 → 493 and shipit 30,333 → 259, app times within A/A. ### SQLite — sparklemotion/sqlite3-ruby

- [#758](https://github.com/sparklemotion/sqlite3-ruby/pull/758), avoid a row Array on statement completion — **one Array/completed statement, not per row**: empty SELECT **2 → 1**, one-row SELECT **4 → 3**, empty UPDATE **2 → 1** objects; repeated DONE stays zero. Allocation-only; no speed, latency or CPU no-regression claim. **Merged 2026-10-26; checked on 2026-11-02.** [Merge 526d4e1](https://github.com/sparklemotion/sqlite3-ruby/commit/526d4e1d8aea588950850d4a204f298616d95b81).

### Prism — ruby/prism

- [#4298](https://github.com/ruby/prism/pull/4298), add Rust `Version::CRuby4_0`, retaining deprecated `CRuby3_5` — both select the same grammar; naming/API compatibility work, **no performance measurement**. Exhaustive matches need the new arm and old-variant uses warn. [Merge ca888b3](https://github.com/ruby/prism/commit/ca888b3a0e715b36a18fb8da0bd89fc7615e9a18).

### Campfire — basecamp/once-campfire

These performance-related PRs were opened before the October 19–22 census.

- [#318](https://github.com/basecamp/once-campfire/pull/318), query sidebar directs and shared rooms separately instead of loading all memberships then splitting/re-sorting — **no benchmark number in the PR body**. [Merge 0d5998f](https://github.com/basecamp/once-campfire/commit/0d5998f05778bf12c73bec3972e3dfa86a5a3af2).
- [#337](https://github.com/basecamp/once-campfire/pull/337), serve bot-page totals from SQLite-maintained `rooms.messages_count`, including bulk/foreign writers — removes the per-page messages `COUNT(*)`; **no measured speedup in the PR body**. This is the trigger-based revision, not the superseded callback counter cache. [Merge fd49099](https://github.com/basecamp/once-campfire/commit/fd490997290b930585b54aa87bde2a7ce1c69204).
- [#339](https://github.com/basecamp/once-campfire/pull/339), move SQLite WAL checkpoints off the request thread to an elected writer with a dedicated connection — **250 ms** PASSIVE loop, unchanged journal mode/`synchronous=NORMAL`; configuration/design evidence, **no benchmark number in the PR body**. [Merge e55b6dc](https://github.com/basecamp/once-campfire/commit/e55b6dc3e534e6dd884eb74eac387fd576c3e6a9).

## Closed

- [rails/rails#60005](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/60005) (UG82D), upgrade-guide note that models can't define a `default_order` scope: since #39525 (unreleased) every model delegates the new `default_order` query method, so `scope :default_order` raises `ArgumentError` at class load and a model's own `def self.default_order` is shadowed on relations. Docs only ([evidence](../../bench/upstream/rails_main_hotlines/ug82d), [item](../../bench/upstream/pr-queue/items/UG82D.md)). **Ready**, opened 2026-11-27 12:01 UTC, head `084c57649f`; thermos-sol and Astra approve. **Converted to draft by Thomas 2026-11-27 13:24 UTC.** **Closed unmerged 2026-11-27 15:59 UTC by rafaelfranca**, after he opened #60019 ("Rename `default_order` to `fallback_order`", commit `71812a394b` references #60005), which he merged at 15:59 UTC; #60019 makes the rename we had offered, so the guide note is no longer needed.
- [rails/rails#60017](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/60017) (TSP1), keep `timestamp_attributes_for_create` and `_for_update` private. Opened ready 2026-11-27, head `25d4cf4f23`. **Closed unmerged 2026-11-27 15:33 UTC by rafaelfranca** ([comment](https://github.com/rails/rails/pull/60017#issuecomment-7222200509): the methods were private, so gems and apps that override them can make their override public).
- [ruby/ruby-bench#579](https://github.com/ruby/ruby-bench/pull/579) (RBCG1): the Callgrind profile's summary line matches its totals; follow-up to Max Bernstein's #576, body references ruby/ruby#19851. **Closed** (was ready), opened 2026-11-14 18:11 UTC, head `d1ab8607b7`, maintainer edits allowed. **2026-11-18 22:36 UTC:** tekknolagi suggested `zero_stats` instead of start/stop (leave it on, zero right before `block.call`), and said he is working on a PR that measures Callgrind instructions per iteration instead of time. **2026-11-19:** his draft #581 does exactly that (zero before each iteration, dump after it); in that order each dump's summary is within a few Ir of its totals (checked with Valgrind; corrected 2026-11-27, first recorded as equal), so #581 covers its purpose but not exactly. Only the exit dump's summary still wraps without a zero after stop. Thomas reacted with a thumbs-up to tekknolagi's comment; #579 stays open without a further comment. **Closed 2026-11-27 13:32 UTC** with one line ([comment](https://github.com/ruby/ruby-bench/pull/579#issuecomment-7219600645)): "Closing in favor of #581, which zeroes before each iteration and dumps before stopping." Decision (coordinator under the two-approvals rule, reversing the 11-19 keep-open): no zero_stats commit, since zero before each block is #581's mechanism and conflicts with it in merge-tree. #581 is the maintainer's superseding design, not an exact equivalent: its per-iteration dumps' summary is a few Ir off totals (-2/-18 on fib, up to about 3.4k with early YJIT, -69..+988 with threads), its exit dump wraps, and --separate-threads=yes fails; its JSON counts read totals and are right. thermos-sol nits and Astra approve the exact line; raw data in bench/upstream/rbcg1/.

  - Original entry: the preloader builds its per-pass working Arrays in place (`Branch#future_classes`, `runnable_loaders`, `Batch#call`) — `Post.preload(:author)` **287 → 277**, three-level preload **1,029 → 982**; preloading workloads in 17 apps median **−70**. From [census 6](rails-main-census-6.md); C6-2 (`immediate_future_classes`/`target_classes` in place) folded in as one commit `58ad783`. Sol approve on both rubrics for each part; opened 2026-11-03. **Switched 2026-11-03 to the simple variant** `2fb7374` (force-pushed with a lease from `58ad783`): plain `.uniq` instead of six `.tap(&:uniq!)`, lib +10/−6, readability L1. Per call: `Post.preload(:comments)` 384 → 375 objects, Ir −0.14% / −0.34% / −0.24% (4.0.7 interpreter / YJIT / master ZJIT; A/A −0.06 / +0.04 / 0.00%); through preload 757 → 730, −0.20% / −0.52% / −0.49% ([evidence](rails-open-evidence.md)). AR sqlite3 10,270 runs, 0 failures. Body updated through REST, no comment. Reviews on `2fb7374`: Sol approve on both rubrics (L1); Astra request-changes with two findings, **accepted** because the exactness checklist accepts overrides of private (`:nodoc:`) methods: (1) a prepended `likely_reflections` that memoizes or freezes its result breaks `target_classes`' `delete_if`/`map!`; (2) a prepended `immediate_future_classes` that freezes or retains its result sees `future_classes` concat into it. Astra's 16 stock and custom-loader cases (polymorphic, through, nested, already loaded) matched base in SQL sequence, loaded graph and order.

Head `9fd1f6f25d`; branch `thomasklemm/kwsplat-fit-merge` kept. **Why closed:** superseded by Kevin Newton's [deaea318b1](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/deaea318b1) (prism treats the `newhash` result as owned and merges the splat in place) and [4a3a75e616](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/commit/4a3a75e616) (zsuper), both 2026-11-09, so these calls allocate one Hash again under both parsers. Rebased onto master `01bace6fce`, the PR would be **+6–9% Ir (calls) and +9–15% (literals) at h.size 0–1**, where about 98% of hits land in the 97-workload census; it wins 13–24% at h.size 2–9, and only under prism. [Closing comment](https://github.com/ruby/ruby/pull/19670#issuecomment-6781235274): "Closing this: deaea318b1 and 4a3a75e616 make these calls allocate one hash again, and the remaining cost at small sizes would get worse with this change. Thanks!" Re-measurement (callgrind with master A/A, size sweep 0–1000, objects and bytes, `#hash` order, tests, reach): [artifact](https://ampcode.com/artifacts/ruby-19670-on-current-master-re-measurement-118743a7-b0b0-4821-900a-fc9d3c57d426).
